\alpha\in\mathfrak{h}_{1+s}
, the objects

F_{\alpha}
,
g_{u,\alpha}
,
\mu_{u,\alpha}
defined in
(
3.4c
)–(
3.4f
) satisfy

(6.11a)

\displaystyle\|F_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{s}}

(6.11b)

\displaystyle\|\nabla F_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1+s}}

(6.11c)

\displaystyle\|g_{u,\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|g_{u,\alpha}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u\|_{H^{1}}\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{s}}

(6.11d)

\displaystyle|\mu_{u,\alpha}|

\displaystyle\leq C\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{s}}^{2}.

Proof.

For the first term in (
6.10a
), applying Young’s
inequality and Parseval,

(6.12)

\displaystyle\|V\ast|u|^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|V\|_{L^{2}}\||u|^{2}\|_{L^{2}}=\|\widehat{V}\|_{L^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:bound:FT:V}}}{{\leq}}C\|u\|_{L^{4}}^{2},

and then the Cauchy-Schwarz and Sobolev inequalities yield

(6.13)

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\leq\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|u\|_{L^{6}}^{3/2}\leq C\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{3/2}.

For the convolution involving
V^{2}
, we proceed similarly, and obtain

(6.14)

\displaystyle\|V^{2}\ast|u|^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|V^{2}\|_{L^{2}}\||u|^{2}\|_{L^{2}}=\|V\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|u\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\leq C\|V\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{3/2}.

By Hausdorff–Young, the Fourier transform is bounded from
L^{4/3}
to

L^{4}
, so by (
6.4a
)

(6.15)

\displaystyle\|V\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|\widehat{V}\|_{L^{4/3}}\leq C.

For the convolution involving the gradient, we use the same inequalities to
estimate

(6.16)

\displaystyle\|\nabla(V\ast|u|^{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 2\|V\|_{L^{4}}\|\overline{u}\nabla u\|_{L^{4/3}}\leq C\|\overline{u}\nabla u\|_{L^{4/3}}.

With Hölder and (
6.13
) we conclude that

(6.17)

\displaystyle\|\overline{u}\nabla u\|_{L^{4/3}}\leq C\|u\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla u\|_{{L^{2}}}\leq C\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{3/2}

and thus obtain (
6.10a
).

In view of
f_{u}(k)=\big\langle u,kB_{(\cdot)}(k)(-i\nabla)u\big\rangle
, the bound on

\|f_{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}
is obvious. To bound the
\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}
-norm of

f_{u}
, we use that

(6.18)

\|f_{u}\sqrt{\omega}\|_{{L^{2}}}\leq 2\|kB_{0}\sqrt{\omega}\|_{L^{4}}\|\mathscr{F}[\overline{u}\nabla u]\|_{L^{4}}.

This implies the bound by Hausdorff-Young inequality
and (
6.17
).

The bounds on
F_{\alpha}(x)=2\mathrm{Re}\big\langle kB_{x},\alpha\big\rangle
and
\nabla F_{\alpha}
follow directly from the from the fact that
|k|B(k)\in\mathfrak{h}_{-s}
.
The bound on
F_{\alpha}
implies that on the
L^{\infty}
-norm of

g_{u,\alpha}=2\big\langle u,kB_{(\cdot)}(k)F_{\alpha}u\big\rangle
. For the

\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}
-norm, writing
g_{u,\alpha}=2kB_{0}\cdot\mathscr{F}[F_{\alpha}|u|^{2}]
gives

\displaystyle\|g_{u,\alpha}\sqrt{\omega}\|_{{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|kB_{0}\sqrt{\omega}\|_{L^{4}}\|\mathscr{F}[F_{\alpha}|u|^{2}]\|_{L^{4}}

(6.19)

\displaystyle\leq C\|F_{\alpha}|u|^{2}\|_{L^{4/3}}\leq C\|F_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}\||u|^{2}\|_{L^{4/3}}.

The claimed bound then follows from (
6.13
) by
Hölder’s inequality, since
\|u\|_{H^{1}}\geq 1
.

The estimate for
\mu_{u,\alpha}=\tfrac{1}{2}\big\langle u,V\ast|u|^{2}u\big\rangle+\mathrm{Re}\big\langle\alpha,f_{u}\big\rangle+\mathrm{Re}\big\langle\alpha,g_{u,\alpha}\big\rangle
follows from
the previous bounds.
∎

We have similar bounds on the mean-field Hamiltonian.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
h_{u,\alpha}
be defined by
(
3.4a
). For every
s>0
there is a constant

C>0
such that for all
(u,\alpha)\in H^{3}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{1+s}
with

\|u\|_{L^{2}}=1

\displaystyle\|h_{u,\alpha}u\|_{{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|u\|_{H^{2}}+\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big)\big(1+\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{s}}^{2}\big),

\displaystyle\|\nabla h_{u,\alpha}u\|_{{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|u\|_{H^{3}}+\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{5/2}\big)\big(1+\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1+s}}\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{s}}^{2}\big).

Proof of Lemma
6.5
.

The proof follows
from Lemmas
6.3
and
6.4
in
combination with (we use
\|\cdot\|_{H^{s}}\geq 1
and

\|\cdot\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{s}}\geq\|\cdot\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{r}}
for
s\geq r
)

\displaystyle\|h_{u,\alpha}u\|_{{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta u\|_{L^{2}}+2\|\big\langle k^{2}B_{(\cdot)},\alpha\big\rangle u\|_{{L^{2}}}+4\big\langle|k|B_{0},|\alpha|\big\rangle\|\nabla u\|_{{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|F_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|V\ast|u|^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}+|\mu_{u,\alpha}|

(6.20)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|u\|_{H^{2}}+\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big)\big(1+\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{s}}^{2},\big)

and

\displaystyle\|\nabla h_{u,\alpha}u\|_{{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\Delta u\|_{L^{2}}+2\|\big\langle k^{3}B_{(\cdot)},\alpha\big\rangle u\|_{{L^{2}}}+6\|\big\langle k^{2}B_{(\cdot)},\alpha\big\rangle\nabla u\|_{{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle\ +4\big\langle|k|B_{0},|\alpha|\big\rangle\|\Delta u\|_{{L^{2}}}+2\|F_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla F_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|F_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\ +\|\nabla(V\ast|u|^{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|V\ast|u|^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla u\|_{{L^{2}}}+|\mu_{u,\alpha}|\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}

(6.21)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|u\|_{H^{3}}+\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{5/2}\big)\big(1+\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1+s}}\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{s}}^{2}\big).

∎

Next, we state suitable bounds for the time-derivatives of
u_{t}
,
\alpha_{t}

and
\mu_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}
. Note that the constant
C
in the bound is
uniform in
t
but not in
(u,\alpha)
, as it depends on the energy of the
initial condition.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
(u,\alpha)\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{5/2}
with
\|u\|_{L^{2}}=1
and
(u_{t},\alpha_{t})=\mathfrak{s}^{\rm D}[t](u,\alpha)
denote the solution to (
3.3
). Let
\mu_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}
be defined as in
(
3.4d
). There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all

|t|\geq 0

\displaystyle\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\dot{\alpha}_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}+|\dot{\mu}_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}|

\displaystyle\leq C\,\|u_{t}\|_{H^{3}}\,\big(1+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{3/2}}\big).

Proof.

We use that
\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}=\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\dot{u}_{t}\|_{{L^{2}}}^{2}
and
i\dot{u}_{t}=h_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}u_{t}
. Since
\|u_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}\leq C
(by
Proposition
2.2
and
Lemma
3.3
), we obtain from Lemma

6.5
for
s=\tfrac{1}{2}

(6.22a)

\displaystyle\|h_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}u_{t}\|_{{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{t}\|_{H^{2}},

(6.22b)

\displaystyle\|\nabla h_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}u_{t}\|_{{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{t}\|_{H^{3}}\big(1+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{3/2}}\big).

With the aid of Lemma
6.4
, one easily verifies

(6.23)

\displaystyle\|\dot{\alpha}_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}\leq\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{3/2}}+\|u_{t}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}.

Recall that
\mu_{u,\alpha}=\tfrac{1}{2}\big\langle u,V\ast|u|^{2}u\big\rangle+\mathrm{Re}\big\langle\alpha,f_{u}\big\rangle+\mathrm{Re}\big\langle\alpha,g_{u,\alpha}\big\rangle
. Since
V
is an
even function,

\displaystyle\big|\tfrac{d}{dt}\big\langle u,V\ast|u|^{2}u\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=4|\mathrm{Re}\big\langle\dot{u}_{t},V\ast|u_{t}|^{2}u_{t}\big\rangle|

(6.24)

\displaystyle\leq 4\|V\ast|u_{t}|^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}.

We further estimate

\displaystyle\|\dot{f}_{u_{t}}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{-1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|kB_{0}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{-1/2}}\left(\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|u_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}\|u_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\leq C\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\|\dot{g}_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{-1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\|\big\langle\dot{u}_{t},kB_{(\cdot)}\cdot F_{\alpha_{t}}u_{t}\big\rangle\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{-1/2}}+2\|\big\langle u_{t},kB_{(\cdot)}\cdot F_{\dot{\alpha}_{t}}u_{t}\big\rangle\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{-1/2}}

(6.25)

\displaystyle\leq\big(2\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|F_{\alpha_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|F_{\dot{\alpha}_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\|kB_{0}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{-1/2}}.

With (
6.4c
) we have
\|F_{\dot{\alpha}_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\dot{\alpha}_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}
, and hence

\displaystyle|\dot{\mu}_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\dot{\alpha}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|f_{u_{t}}+g_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}\|\dot{f}_{u_{t}}+\dot{g}_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{-1/2}}

(6.26)

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\dot{\alpha}_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}+\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}).

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

The next lemma summarizes estimates for the different kernels (and their
time-derivatives) that appear in the (dressed) Bogoliubov Hamiltonian
(
4.1
) and the fluctuation Hamiltonian introduced in
(
3.8
), given explicitly by
(
6.68
).

We introduce the integral kernels

(6.27a)

\displaystyle N_{u}(x,k,l)

\displaystyle=(q_{u}kB_{(\cdot)}(k)\cdot lB_{(\cdot)}(l)u)(x),

(6.27b)

\displaystyle Q_{u}(x,y,k,l)

\displaystyle=(q_{u}kB_{(\cdot)}(k)\cdot lB_{(\cdot)}(l)q_{u})(x,y),

where we note that
kB_{x}(k)\cdot lB_{x}(l)
acts as a multiplication
operator in
x
. Recalling the definition
\big(L_{\alpha}(k)f\big)(x)=2kB_{x}(k)\big((-i\nabla+F_{\alpha}(x))f\big)(x)

from (
4.2a
), we set

(6.28)

\displaystyle\ell_{t}^{(1)}(x,k)

\displaystyle=(q_{u_{t}}L_{\alpha_{t}}(k)u_{t})(x),\quad\ell_{t}^{(2)}(x,k)=(q_{u_{t}}L_{\alpha_{t}}(k)^{*}u_{t})(x).

Moreover, for
u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
\{u\}^{\perp}\subset L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, we consider the operators

\displaystyle K_{u}^{(3)}

\displaystyle:\{u\}^{\perp}\rightarrow\{u\}^{\perp}\otimes\{u\}^{\perp}

(6.29a)

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle\mapsto(K_{u}^{(3)}\psi)(x_{1},x_{2})=(q_{u})_{1}(q_{u})_{2}W_{u}(x_{1},x_{2})u(x_{1})(q_{u}\psi)(x_{2}),

\displaystyle K_{u}^{(4)}

\displaystyle:\{u\}^{\perp}\otimes\{u\}^{\perp}\rightarrow\{u\}^{\perp}\otimes\{u\}^{\perp}

(6.29b)

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle\mapsto(K_{u}^{(4)}\psi)(x_{1},x_{2})=(q_{u})_{1}(q_{u})_{2}W_{u}(x_{1},x_{2})(q_{u}\otimes q_{u}\psi)(x_{1},x_{2}).

with, for
V
defined in (
3.2b
),

(6.30)

\displaystyle W_{u}(x_{1},x_{2})=V(x_{1}-x_{2})-V\ast|u|^{2}(x_{1})-V\ast|u|^{2}(x_{2})+\big\langle u,V\ast|u|^{2}u\big\rangle.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
(u,\alpha)\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{5/2}
with
\|u\|_{L^{2}}=1
, denote by
(u_{t},\alpha_{t})=\mathfrak{s}^{\rm D}[t](u,\alpha)
the solution to (
3.3
),
and
\rho(t)=\|u_{t}\|_{H^{3}}^{2}\,(1+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{3/2}})^{2}
. There exists a constant
C>0
such that for
all
|t|\geq 0

(6.31a)

\displaystyle\|K_{u_{t}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}

\displaystyle\leq C\hskip 56.9055pt\|\dot{K}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}

(6.31b)

\displaystyle\|K_{u_{t}}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\hskip 56.9055pt\|\dot{K}_{u_{t}}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}

(6.31c)

\displaystyle\|\ell_{t}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\hskip 56.9055pt\|\dot{\ell}_{t}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}

(6.31d)

\displaystyle\|\ell_{t}^{(2)}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/4}\to L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\hskip 56.9055pt\|\dot{\ell}_{t}^{(2)}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/4}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}

(6.31e)

\displaystyle\|M_{u_{t}}\|_{(L^{2})^{\otimes 2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\hskip 56.9055pt\|\dot{M}_{u_{t}}\|_{(L^{2})^{\otimes 2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}

(6.31f)

\displaystyle\|K_{u_{t}}^{(3)}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}\otimes L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\hskip 56.9055pt\|\dot{K}_{u_{t}}^{(3)}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}\otimes L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}

(6.31g)

\displaystyle\|N_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/8}^{\otimes 2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\hskip 56.9055pt\|\dot{N}_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/8}^{\otimes 2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}

(6.31h)

\displaystyle\|Q_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{1/8}^{\otimes 2}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\hskip 56.9055pt\|\dot{Q}_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{1/8}^{\otimes 2}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}

where
L^{2}
stands for
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

Let us note the evident fact that the bounds (
6.31a
) –
(
6.31e
) hold uniformly also for the
\Lambda
-dependent
kernels introduced in (
5.10
). For instance,

\|K^{(2),\Lambda}_{1,u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\leq C
for all

\Lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{\infty\}
with
K^{(2),\Lambda}_{1,u_{t}}

defined by (
5.11e
). While we do not state them explicitly
here, such uniform bounds will be used in the proofs of Lemmas

6.8
and
6.9
.

Proof.

Recall that
\|u_{t}\|_{L^{2}}=1
and
\|u_{t}\|_{H_{1}}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}\leq C
for all
|t|\geq 0
by
Proposition
2.2
and
Lemma
3.3
. We go through the claimed bounds
line-by-line.

Line (
6.31a
)
. We use that

\|q_{u_{t}}\|=1
, so
\|K_{u_{t}}^{(2)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\leq\|\widetilde{K}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}
and

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{K}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}

\displaystyle=\int dxdy\,|u_{t}(x)|^{2}V^{2}(x-y)|u_{t}(y)|^{2}

(6.32)

\displaystyle\leq\|V^{2}\ast|u_{t}|^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|u_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C

by Lemma
6.4
. Invoking

(6.33)

\displaystyle\dot{K}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\dot{q}_{u_{t}}\otimes q_{u_{t}}\widetilde{K}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}+q_{u_{t}}\otimes\dot{q}_{u_{t}}\widetilde{K}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}+q_{u_{t}}\otimes q_{u_{t}}\tfrac{d}{dt}{\widetilde{K}}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}

together with
\dot{q}_{u_{t}}=-\dot{p}_{u_{t}}
,
\|\dot{p}_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\rightarrow L^{2}}\leq 2\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{{L^{2}}}
and

\|\tfrac{d}{dt}\widetilde{K}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\leq C\ \|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{{L^{2}}}
as in (
6.32
), we obtain

(6.34)

\displaystyle\|\dot{K}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\leq C\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{{L^{2}}}\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)},

where we employed Lemma
6.6
in the last
step.

Line (
6.31b
)
. Since

|K_{u_{t}}^{(1)}(x,y)|=|K^{(2)}_{u_{t}}(x,y)|
we can use that the
operator norm is bounded by the Hilbert–Schmidt norm,

\|K_{u_{t}}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\leq\|K_{u_{t}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}
, so that we can apply the previous bounds. The time-derivative is
bounded analogously.

Line (
6.31c
)
. We recall
\ell_{t}^{(1)}(x,k)=(q_{u_{t}}kB_{(\cdot)}(k)\cdot(-i\nabla+F_{\alpha_{t}})u_{t})(x)

and estimate

\displaystyle\|\ell_{t}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|kB_{0}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}}\|(-i\nabla+F_{\alpha_{t}})u_{t}\|_{{L^{2}}}

(6.35)

\displaystyle\leq C(\|u_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|F_{\alpha_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}})\leq C,

and similarly for

(6.36)

\displaystyle\|\dot{\ell}_{t}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|kB_{0}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}}\|\tfrac{d}{dt}q_{u_{t}}e^{-ik(\cdot)}(-i\nabla+F_{\alpha_{t}})u_{t}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|F_{\dot{\alpha}_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}})\leq C(\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\dot{\alpha}_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}})\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)},

where we used
\|\dot{q}_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\leq 2\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq 2\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}
and
\|F_{\dot{\alpha}_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\dot{\alpha}_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}
.

Line (
6.31d
)
. We have

(6.37)

\ell_{t}^{(2)}(x,k)=\ell^{(1)}_{t}(x,k)+2(q_{u_{t}}k^{2}B_{(\cdot)}(k)u_{t}\big)(x),

and thus

\displaystyle\|\ell^{(2)}_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/4}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\ell^{(1)}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/4}\to L^{2}}+2\sup_{\|\eta\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/4}}=1}\Big\|\int dk\,k^{2}B_{(\cdot)}(k)\eta(k)u_{t}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

(6.38)

\displaystyle\leq\|\ell^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}}+C\|u_{t}\|_{H^{1}}

by (
6.4d
) (with
\alpha=\eta
,
u=u_{t}
,
n=2
and

s=1/4
). Similarly, for the time-derivative

(6.39)

\displaystyle\|\dot{\ell}_{t}^{(2)}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/4}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\dot{\ell}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}}+C\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}.

Line (
6.31e
)
. The estimate

\displaystyle\left|M_{u_{t}}(k,l)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{kB_{0}(k)\cdot lB_{0}(l)(2\pi)^{3/2}(k+l)\cdot\mathcal{F}[i\nabla\left|u_{t}\right|^{2}](k+l)}{\left|k+l\right|^{2}}\right|

(6.40)

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{t}\nabla u_{t}\|_{L^{1}}\frac{\left|k\right|B_{0}(k)\left|l\right|B_{0}(l)}{\left|k+l\right|}

and the Hardy–Littlewood–Sobolev inequality imply

(6.41)

\displaystyle\|M_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{t}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\|\left|\cdot\right|B_{0}\|_{L^{3}}^{2}\leq C\|u_{t}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

For
\dot{M}_{u_{t}}
we obtain by the same argument

(6.42)

\|\dot{M}_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\leq C\|u_{t}\|_{H^{1}}\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{H^{1}},

which implies the claim by Lemma
6.6
.

Line (
6.31f
)
. Recall the definition of

W_{u}
in (
6.30
), and denote
q_{i}=(q_{u_{t}})_{i}
. Using

\|u_{t}\|_{H^{1}}\leq C
we get

\displaystyle\|K^{(3)}_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}\otimes L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|\psi\|=1}\|q_{1}q_{2}W_{u_{t}}(x_{1},x_{2})u_{t}(x_{1})(q_{u_{t}}\psi)(x_{2})\|_{L^{2}\otimes L^{2}}

(6.43)

\displaystyle\leq C\|q_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}^{3}\big(\|V^{2}\ast|u_{t}|^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/2}+\|V\ast|u_{t}|^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\leq C

by Lemma
6.4
. For the norm of the time-derivative, one
computes

\displaystyle(\dot{K}^{(3)}_{u_{t}}\psi)(x_{1},x_{2})=q_{1}q_{2}\dot{W}_{u_{t}}(x_{1},x_{2})u_{t}(x_{1})(q\psi)(x_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\dot{q}_{1}q_{2}W_{u_{t}}(x_{1},x_{2})u_{t}(x_{1})(q\psi)(x_{2})+q_{1}\dot{q}_{2}W_{u_{t}}(x_{1},x_{2})u_{t}(x_{1})(q\psi)(x_{2})

(6.44)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+q_{1}q_{2}W_{u_{t}}(x_{1},x_{2})\dot{u}_{t}(x_{1})(q\psi)(x_{2})+q_{1}q_{2}W_{u_{t}}(x_{1},x_{2})u_{t}(x_{1})(\dot{q}\psi)(x_{2})

where each term can be estimated similarly as in (
6.2
). Using
Lemma
6.4
in

(6.45)

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{d}{dt}V\ast\left|u_{t}\right|^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 2\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|V^{2}\ast|u_{t}|^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/2}\leq C\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{{L^{2}}},

one obtains
\|\dot{W}_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\leq C\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{{L^{2}}}
. Together with
\|\dot{q}_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\leq 2\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}
, this leads to
\|\dot{K}^{(3)}_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}\otimes L^{2}}\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}
.

Lines (
6.31g
) and
(
6.31h
)
. Recalling the definitions of
N_{t},Q_{t}
in
(
6.27a
), (
6.27b
) it follows readily that

(6.46a)

\displaystyle\|N_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/8}^{\otimes 2}}+\|Q_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{1/8}^{\otimes 2}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|kB_{0}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{-1/8}}^{2}\leq C

(6.46b)

\displaystyle\|\dot{N}_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/8}^{\otimes 2}}+\|\dot{Q}_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{1/8}^{\otimes 2}\to L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|kB_{0}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{-1/8}}^{2}\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

6.3.
Estimates for the Bogoliubov
Hamiltonians

The first Lemma provides bounds on the dressed Bogoliubov Hamiltonian
(
4.1
) and its time-derivative, its difference to the
operator
\mathbb{T}=\text{d}\Gamma_{b}(-\Delta)+\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega)
, and its commutator with the total number operator

\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{b}+\mathcal{N}_{a}
. These imply existence and
uniqueness of the associated dynamics, as explained in
Proposition
5.2
. Similar bounds also hold for the family of
interpolating Bogoliubov Hamiltonians (
5.10
) (recall that
\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha}^{\rm D}(t)=\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha,1}^{\infty}(t)
).
Note that for
\theta=1
the bounds in part (b) are uniform in
\Lambda
.

Lemma 6.8
.

(a)

Let
(u,\alpha)\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{5/2}
with

\|u\|_{L^{2}}=1
and
(u_{t},\alpha_{t})=\mathfrak{s}^{\rm D}[t](u,\alpha)

denote the solution to (
3.3
). There exists a constant

C>0
such that for all
t\in\mathbb{R}

(6.47a)

\displaystyle\pm\big(\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha}^{\rm D}(t)-\mathbb{T}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{T}+C(\mathcal{N}+1)

(6.47b)

\displaystyle\pm i[\mathcal{N},\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha}^{\rm D}(t)]

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{T}+C(\mathcal{N}+1)

(6.47c)

\displaystyle\pm\tfrac{d}{dt}\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha}^{\rm D}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{T}+C\rho(t)(\mathcal{N}+1)

as quadratic forms on
\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}
, where
\rho(t)=\|u_{t}\|_{H^{3}}^{2}(1+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{3/2}})^{2}
.

(b)
Let
(u,\alpha)\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{5/2}
with
\|u\|_{L^{2}}=1
and
(u_{t},\alpha_{t})=\mathfrak{s}_{\theta}[t](u,\alpha)
as defined in
(
5.1
). There exists a constant
C>0
such that for
all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
|\theta|\leq 1
and
\Lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{+}

(6.48a)

\displaystyle\pm\big(\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha,\theta}^{\Lambda}(t)-\mathbb{T}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{T}+C(1+|1-\theta|\Lambda)(\mathcal{N}+1)

(6.48b)

\displaystyle\pm i[\mathcal{N},\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha,\theta}^{\Lambda}(t)]

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{T}+C(1+|1-\theta|\Lambda)(\mathcal{N}+1)

(6.48c)

\displaystyle\pm\tfrac{d}{dt}\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha,\theta}^{\Lambda}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{T}+C(1+|1-\theta|\Lambda)\rho(t)(\mathcal{N}+1)

as quadratic forms on
\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}
, where
\rho(t)=\|u_{t}\|_{H^{3}}^{2}(1+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{3/2}})^{2}
.

Proof.

The proof follows essentially by combining the operator bounds
of Lemma
6.1
with the kernel bounds of
Lemma
6.7
. We give the details below.

Proof of (
6.47a
)
. Recall that

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{\rm D}_{u,\alpha}(t)-\mathbb{T}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}\big(A_{\alpha_{t}}+F^{2}_{\alpha_{t}}-\mu_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}\big)+\mathbb{K}_{u_{t}}^{(1)}+\big(\mathbb{K}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}+\text{h.c.}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(\int dkdx\Big(\ell_{t}^{(1)}(x,k)b_{x}^{*}a_{k}^{*}+\ell_{t}^{(2)}(x,k)b_{x}^{*}a_{k}\Big)+\text{h.c.}\bigg)

(6.49)

\displaystyle\quad+\int dkdl\,M_{u_{t}}(k,l)\,\mathcal{A}_{kl}.

Since
A_{\alpha_{t}}=2(-i\nabla_{x})\cdot\big\langle kB_{x},\alpha_{t}\big\rangle+\text{h.c.}
the first term in the first line is bounded, using the
Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, by

\displaystyle\pm\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}\big(A_{\alpha_{t}}+F^{2}_{\alpha_{t}}-\mu_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\varepsilon\text{d}\Gamma_{b}(-\Delta)+\Big(\frac{4}{\varepsilon}\big\langle|k|B_{0},|\alpha_{t}|\big\rangle^{2}+\|F_{\alpha_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+|\mu_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}|\Big)\mathcal{N}_{b}

(6.50)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\varepsilon\text{d}\Gamma_{b}(-\Delta)+\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{N}_{b},

where the last bound follows from Lemmas
6.3
and

6.4
, and
\|u_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}\leq C
.

For the second and third term in the first line, we apply Lemmas

6.1
and
6.7
to get

(6.51a)

\displaystyle\pm\mathbb{K}_{u_{t}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\leq\|K_{u_{t}}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)\leq C(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1),

(6.51b)

\displaystyle\pm(\mathbb{K}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}+\text{h.c.})

\displaystyle\leq 2\|K_{u_{t}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)\leq C(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1).

For the mixed quadratic terms, we use the Lemma
6.1
choosing

m_{a}=m_{b}=0
,
n_{a}=n_{b}=1
,
s=1/2
and
r_{a}=0
,
r_{b}=1
for the term
involving
b_{x}^{*}a_{k}^{*}
, and
m_{a}=1=n_{b}
,
n_{a}=m_{b}=0
,
t=1/2
and

r_{a}=r_{b}=0
for the term involving
b_{x}^{*}a_{k}
. Thus gives

\displaystyle\pm\bigg(\int dkdx\Big(\ell^{(1)}_{t}(x,k)b_{x}^{*}a_{k}^{*}+\ell^{(2)}_{t}(x,k)b_{x}^{*}a_{k}\Big)+\text{h.c.}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\leq\varepsilon\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega)+C\varepsilon^{-1}\Big(\|\ell_{t}^{(1)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/2}}+\|\ell_{t}^{(2)}\|^{2}_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}\to L^{2}}\Big)(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)

(6.52)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\leq\varepsilon\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega)+C\varepsilon^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1),

where we used again Lemma
6.7
and monotonicity of the

\mathfrak{h}_{s}
-norms.

In the last term in (
6.49
) we have
\mathcal{A}_{kl}=-2a_{k}^{*}a_{-l}+a_{k}^{*}a_{l}^{*}+a_{-l}a_{-k}
, so using that the operator
norm is bounded by the Hilbert-Schmidt norm, we have with
Lemma
6.1
and (
6.31e
)

(6.53)

\displaystyle\pm\int dkdl\ M_{u_{t}}(k,l)\mathcal{A}_{k,l}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{N}_{a}+1).

Proof of (
6.47b
)
. The
commutator is easily found to be

(6.54)

[\mathcal{N},\mathbb{H}^{\rm D}_{u,\alpha}(t)]=2\bigg(\int dkdx\,\ell_{t}^{(1)}(x,k)b_{x}^{*}a_{k}^{*}-\text{h.c.}\bigg)\\
+2\int dkdl\,M_{u_{t}}(k,l)\,(a^{*}_{k}a_{l}^{*}-a_{-k}a_{-l}),

which can be estimated exactly as in (
6.3
) and
(
6.53
).

Proof of (
6.47c
)
. We compute

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{H}^{\rm D}_{u,\alpha}(t)

\displaystyle=\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}(\dot{h}_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}})+\dot{\mathbb{K}}_{u_{t}}^{(1)}+(\dot{\mathbb{K}}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}+\text{h.c.})

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(\int dkdx\Big(\dot{\ell}^{(1)}_{t}(x,k)b_{x}^{*}a_{k}^{*}+\dot{\ell}^{(2)}_{t}(x,k)b_{x}^{*}a_{k}\Big)+\text{h.c.}\bigg)

(6.55)

\displaystyle\quad+\int dkdl\,\dot{M}_{u_{t}}(k,l)\,\mathcal{A}_{kl},

with

(6.56)

\displaystyle\dot{h}_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}=A_{\dot{\alpha}_{t}}+2F_{\alpha_{t}}\cdot F_{\dot{\alpha}_{t}}+\tfrac{d}{dt}V\ast\left|u_{t}\right|^{2}-\dot{\mu}_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}.

Since
A_{\dot{\alpha}_{t}}=2(-i\nabla_{x})\cdot\big\langle kB_{x},\dot{\alpha}_{t}\big\rangle+\text{h.c.}
we can use Cauchy–Schwarz and Lemma

6.3
to obtain

(6.57)

\displaystyle\pm\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}(A_{\dot{\alpha}_{t}})\leq\varepsilon\text{d}\Gamma_{b}(-\Delta)+\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\|\dot{\alpha}_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}^{2}\,\mathcal{N}_{b}.

Recalling
F_{\dot{\alpha}_{t}}(x)=2\mathrm{Re}\big\langle kB_{x},\dot{\alpha}_{t}\big\rangle
we
estimate

(6.58)

\displaystyle\pm\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}(F_{\alpha_{t}}\cdot F_{\dot{\alpha}_{t}})

\displaystyle\leq\|F_{\alpha_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|F_{\dot{\alpha}_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\mathcal{N}_{b}\leq C\|\dot{\alpha}_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}\,\mathcal{N}_{b}.

The time-derivative of the convolution is estimated in
(
6.45
), and thus

(6.59)

\displaystyle\pm\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}(\tfrac{d}{dt}V\ast|u_{t}|^{2})\leq C\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\,\mathcal{N}_{b}.

Combining the above estimates, we arrive at

(6.60)

\displaystyle\pm\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}(\dot{h}_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}})

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\text{d}\Gamma_{b}(-\Delta)+\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\Big(\|\dot{\alpha}_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}}^{2}+\|\dot{u}_{t}\|_{{L^{2}}}\Big)\mathcal{N}_{b}.

Similarly as in (
6.51a
) and (
6.51b
)
we bound the remaining terms in the first line in (
6.3
)

(6.61a)

\displaystyle\pm\dot{\mathbb{K}}_{u_{t}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\leq\|\dot{K}_{u_{t}}^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\rightarrow L^{2}}\left(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1\right)\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}\left(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1\right),

(6.61b)

\displaystyle\pm(\dot{\mathbb{K}}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}+\text{h.c.})

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\dot{K}_{u_{t}}^{(2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\left(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1\right)\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}\left(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1\right).

By the reasoning of (
6.3
) we obtain

(6.62)

\pm\bigg(\int dkdx\Big({\dot{\ell}_{t}^{(1)}}(x,k)b_{x}^{*}a_{k}^{*}+{{\dot{\ell}_{t}^{(2)}}(x,k)}b_{x}^{*}a_{k}\Big)+\text{h.c.}\bigg)\\
\leq\varepsilon\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega)+\frac{C\rho(t)}{\varepsilon}\left(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1\right),

and analogously to (
6.53
) one also verifies that

(6.63)

\displaystyle\pm\int dkdl\,\dot{M}_{u_{t}}(k,l)\,\mathcal{A}_{kl}

\displaystyle\leq C\rho(t)\,\left(\mathcal{N}_{a}+1\right).

The above estimates prove (
6.47c
) and thus
complete the proof of part (a) of the lemma.

Turning to part (b), first note that for
\theta=1
the proof is verbatim
the same as the one for part (a). To see this, recall that all kernels that
appear in
\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha,1}^{\Lambda}(t)
satisfy the same bounds as in
Lemma
6.7
, as explained thereafter (in particular, they
are uniformly bounded in
\Lambda
).

The crucial difference for
\theta\neq 1
, apart from the trivial

\theta
-dependence of the kernels, is the appearance of the term

(6.64)

\displaystyle(1-\theta)\int dkdx\,\bigg((q_{u_{t}}G_{x}^{\Lambda}(k)u_{t})a_{k}^{*}b_{x}^{*}+(q_{u_{t}}\overline{G_{x}^{\Lambda}(k)}u_{t})a_{k}^{*}b_{x}\bigg)+\text{h.c.}

in
\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha,\theta}^{\Lambda}
which is not present when

\theta=1
(hence this term did not appear in the proof of part (a)). Since

\|q_{u_{t}}G_{(\cdot)}^{\Lambda}u_{t}\|_{L^{2}\otimes L^{2}}\leq\|G_{0}^{\Lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\Lambda
, we can bound the above term by
C\Lambda(\mathcal{N}+1)
. All other contributions in
\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha,\theta}^{\Lambda}(t)
are estimated as for
\theta=1
, i.e. they
are uniformly bounded in
\Lambda
. This explains the
\Lambda
-dependent
upper bound in (
6.47a
).
The bounds for the commutator and the time-derivative are obtained in the
same way. ∎

The next lemma was used in the proof of Proposition
5.2
to
show that
\mathbb{U}_{1}^{\Lambda}(t)\to\mathbb{U}_{1}^{\infty}(t)
strongly as

\Lambda\to\infty
.

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
(u,\alpha)\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{5/2}
with

\|u\|_{L^{2}}=1
,
(u_{t},\alpha_{t})=\mathfrak{s}_{1}[t](u,\alpha)
as defined
in (
5.1
) and
\rho(t)=\|u_{t}\|_{H^{3}}^{2}(1+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{3/2}})^{2}
. There is a family
\varepsilon_{\Lambda}>0

with
\varepsilon_{\Lambda}\xrightarrow{\Lambda\to\infty}0
such that

\big|\big\langle\chi,(\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha,1}^{\infty}(t)-\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha,1}^{\Lambda}(t))\phi\big\rangle\big|\\
\leq\varepsilon_{\Lambda}e^{C\int_{0}^{|t|}\rho(s)ds}\|(\mathbb{T}+\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\chi\|\|(\mathbb{T}+\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\phi\|

for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
\chi,\phi\in D((\mathbb{T}+\mathcal{N})^{1/2})
.

Proof.

The difference
\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha,1}^{\infty}(t)-\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha,1}^{\Lambda}(t)
is up to the term

\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}(h_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}})+\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega)

precisely of the same form as (
5.10
) with all kernels
replaced by kernels of the form
K_{u_{t},1}^{(1),\infty}-K_{u_{t},1}^{(1),\Lambda}
and
M^{\infty}_{u_{t},1}(k,l)-M_{u_{t},1}^{\Lambda}(k,l)
, and analogously for the other terms. The claimed bound
is now obtained following the same steps as in the proof of
(
6.47a
) and taking into account that, by Lemma

6.7
and continuity in
\Lambda
, the norms of the kernel
differences all vanish as
\Lambda\to\infty
.
∎

6.4.
Fluctuation generator for the dressed dynamics

We start by stating the precise form of the fluctuation generator
H^{\rm D}_{u,\alpha}(t)
introduced in (
3.8
). For

[a]_{+}=\max\{0,a\}
and
\mathfrak{s}^{\rm D}[t](u,\alpha)
set

\displaystyle H_{0}(t)

\displaystyle=\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega)+\text{d}\Gamma_{b}(h_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}})+\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}\big]_{+}\mathbb{K}^{(1)}_{u_{t}}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\mathbb{K}^{(2)}_{u_{t}}\frac{\sqrt{[(N-\mathcal{N}_{b})(N-\mathcal{N}_{b}-1)]_{+}}}{N}+\text{h.c.}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\int dkdx(q_{u_{t}}L_{\alpha_{t}}(k)u_{t})(x)\,b_{x}^{*}a_{k}^{*}\sqrt{[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}]_{+}}+\textnormal{h.c.}

\displaystyle\quad+\int dkdx(q_{u_{t}}L_{\alpha_{t}}(k)^{*}u_{t})(x)b_{x}^{*}a_{k}\sqrt{[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}]_{+}}+\textnormal{h.c.}

(6.65)

\displaystyle\quad+\int dkdl\,M_{u_{t}}(k,l)\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}\big]_{+}\,\big(-2a^{*}_{k}a_{-l}+a_{k}^{*}a_{l}^{*}+a_{-k}a_{-l}\big)

with
L_{\alpha_{t}}(k)
,
M_{u_{t}}(k,l)
and
\mathbb{K}^{(j)}_{u_{t}}
defined
in (
4.2a
), (
3.4a
) and
(
4.2c
), respectively. Note that up to the
N
-dependent factors

H_{0}(t)
coincides with
\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha}^{\rm D}(t)
. We further
introduce the operators

(6.66a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{K}^{(3)}_{u_{t}}

\displaystyle=\int dx_{1}dx_{2}dx_{3}\,K_{u_{t}}^{(3)}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})b^{*}_{x_{1}}b^{*}_{x_{2}}b_{x_{3}}

(6.66b)

\displaystyle\mathbb{K}^{(4)}_{u_{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int dx_{1}dx_{2}dx_{3}dx_{4}\,K_{u_{t}}^{(4)}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})b^{*}_{x_{1}}b^{*}_{x_{2}}b_{x_{3}}b_{x_{4}},

where for
u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
\{u\}^{\perp}\subset L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
we used the kernels of the operators
K_{u}^{(3)}
,
K_{u}^{(4)}

introduced in (
6.29a
), (
6.29b
). Lastly, recall
(
6.27a
) and (
6.27b
) and let

(6.67)

\displaystyle J_{u,\alpha}(x,k,y)

\displaystyle=2(q_{u}kB_{(\cdot)}(k)\cdot(-i\nabla+F_{\alpha})q_{u})(x,y).

The proof of the next lemma follows from a straightforward computation, which
is postponed to Appendix
C
.

Lemma 6.10
.

For
(u,\alpha)\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{5/2}
with
\|u\|_{L^{2}}=1
let
(u_{t},\alpha_{t})=\mathfrak{s}^{\mathrm{D}}[t](u,\alpha)
be the solution to (
3.3
). The operator
H_{u,\alpha}^{{\rm D},\leq N}(t):\mathcal{F}^{\leq N}_{\perp u_{t}}\otimes\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}
defined by
(
3.7
) satisfies the identity
H^{{\rm D},\leq N}_{u,\alpha}(t)=H_{u,\alpha}^{\rm D}(t)\restriction\mathcal{F}^{\leq N}_{\perp u_{t}}\otimes\mathcal{F}
where
H^{\rm D}_{u,\alpha}(t):\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}
is given by

(6.68)

\displaystyle H_{u,\alpha}^{\rm D}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{5}H_{j}(t)

with
H_{0}(t)
defined by (
6.65
), and

(6.69a)

\displaystyle H_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} &-\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{d}\Gamma(V\ast|u_{t}|^{2}-\mu_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}})\\
&\quad+\Big(\mathbb{K}^{(3)}_{u_{t}}\frac{\sqrt{[N-\mathcal{N}_{b}]_{+}}}{N}+\textnormal{h.c.}\Big)+\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{K}^{(4)}_{u_{t}},\end{aligned}

(6.69b)

\displaystyle H_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\mathcal{N}_{b}\hat{\Phi}(f_{u_{t}}+g_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}),

(6.69c)

\displaystyle H_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dkdxdy\,J_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}(x,k,y)\,b_{x}^{*}b_{y}\,a_{k}^{*}+\textnormal{h.c.},

(6.69d)

\displaystyle H_{4}(t)

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dkdldx\Big(&N_{u_{t}}(x,k,l)\,b_{x}^{*}\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}\big]_{+}^{1/2}\\
&+\overline{N_{u_{t}}(x,k,l)}\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}\big]_{+}^{1/2}b_{x}\Big)\mathcal{A}_{kl},\end{aligned}

(6.69e)

\displaystyle H_{5}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\int dkdldxdy\,Q_{u_{t}}(x,y,k,l)\,b_{x}^{*}b_{y}\,\mathcal{A}_{kl},

where
\mathcal{A}_{kl}=-2a^{*}_{k}a_{-l}+a_{k}^{*}a_{l}^{*}+a_{-k}a_{-l}
.

The next lemma provides estimates for the fluctuation Hamiltonian that are an
important ingredient of the proof of Theorem
3.2
.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
(u,\alpha)\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{5/2}
with
\|u\|_{L^{2}}=1
,

(u_{t},\alpha_{t})=\mathfrak{s}^{\rm D}[t](u,\alpha)
denote the solution to
(
3.3
). There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all

t\in\mathbb{R}

(6.70a)

\displaystyle\pm\big(H^{\rm D}_{u,\alpha}(t)-\mathbb{T}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{T}+C(\mathcal{N}+1)(1+\tfrac{1}{N}\mathcal{N}_{b})^{2}

(6.70b)

\displaystyle\pm i[\mathcal{N},H^{\rm D}_{u,\alpha}(t)]

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{T}+C(\mathcal{N}+1)(1+\tfrac{1}{N}\mathcal{N}_{b})^{2}

(6.70c)

\displaystyle\pm\tfrac{d}{dt}H^{\rm D}_{u,\alpha}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{T}+C\rho(t)(\mathcal{N}+1)(1+\tfrac{1}{N}\mathcal{N}_{b})^{2}.

as quadratic forms on
\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}
, where
\rho(t)=\|u_{t}\|^{2}_{H^{3}}(1+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{3/2}})^{2}
.

Proof of (
6.70a
).

Comparing
(
6.65
) with (
6.49
) we see that
H_{0}(t)

differs from
\mathbb{H}^{\rm D}_{u,\alpha}(t)
only by the factors

(6.71)

\displaystyle 0\leq\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}\big]_{+}\leq 1,\quad 0\leq\tfrac{\sqrt{[(N-\mathcal{N}_{b})(N-\mathcal{N}_{b}-1)]_{+}}}{N}\leq 1

and is thus estimated in analogy to the proof of Lemma

6.8
.

For later purpose we consider
\big\langle\chi,H_{1}(t)\phi\big\rangle
for
\chi,\phi\in\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}
. The first two terms in
H_{1}(t)
are
estimated by

(6.72)

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\chi,\frac{1}{N}\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}\big(V\ast|u_{t}|^{2}-\mu_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}\big)\phi\big\rangle\big|\leq\frac{C}{N}\|\mathcal{N}_{b}^{1/2}\chi\|\,\|\mathcal{N}_{b}^{1/2}\phi\|,

where we used Lemma
6.4
, and

\displaystyle\Big|\big\langle\chi,\Big(\mathbb{K}^{(3)}_{u_{t}}\tfrac{\sqrt{[N-\mathcal{N}_{b}]_{+}}}{N}+\text{h.c.}\Big)\phi\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\|K^{(3)}_{u_{t}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}\otimes L^{2}}\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)^{1/2}\chi\|\,\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)\phi\|

(6.73)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\tfrac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)^{1/2}\chi\|\,\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)\phi\|,

by Lemmas
6.1
and
6.7
.

To estimate the term involving
\mathbb{K}_{u_{t}}^{(4)}
we recall
(
3.2b
) and write
W_{u_{t}}(x,y)=W^{{\rm b}}_{u_{t}}(x,y)-4\mathrm{Re}\big\langle G_{x},B_{y}\big\rangle
, where
W_{u_{t}}^{{\rm b}}(x,y)

is point-wise bounded. Using the symmetry of
\chi^{(n)},\phi^{(n)}\in\mathcal{F}^{(n)}\otimes\mathcal{F}
in the
n
particle coordinates, we
find

(6.74)

\big\langle\chi,\mathbb{K}_{u_{t}}^{(4)}\phi\big\rangle\\
=\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\big\langle q_{1}q_{2}\chi^{(n)},\big(W_{u_{t}}^{\rm{b}}(x_{1},x_{2})-4\mathrm{Re}\big\langle G_{x_{1}},B_{x_{2}}\big\rangle\big)q_{1}q_{2}\phi^{(n)}\big\rangle

with

(6.75)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\big\langle q_{1}q_{2}\chi^{(n)},W_{u_{t}}^{\rm{b}}(x_{1},x_{2})q_{1}q_{2}\phi^{(n)}\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathcal{N}_{b}^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{b}^{\frac{3}{2}}\phi\|.

We use the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the fact that

(6.76)

e^{ikx}=(1+(-i\nabla_{1}-k)^{2})^{-1/2}e^{ikx}(1-\Delta_{1})^{1/2}

to bound the remaining term by

(6.77)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\big\langle q_{1}q_{2}\chi^{(n)},\mathrm{Re}\big\langle G_{x_{1}},B_{x_{2}}\big\rangle q_{1}q_{2}\phi^{(n)}\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n^{3}\|\phi^{(n)}\|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n\|\mathrm{Re}\big\langle G_{x_{1}},B_{x_{2}}\big\rangle q_{1}q_{2}\chi^{(n)}\|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathcal{N}_{b}^{3/2}\phi\|

\displaystyle\ \times\Big(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n\Big\|\int dk(1+(-i\nabla_{1}-k)^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\overline{G_{x_{1}}(k)}B_{x_{2}}(k)(1-\Delta_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}q_{1}q_{2}\chi^{(n)}\Big\|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The last factor is bounded by

(6.78)

\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\{\int dk\,\frac{G_{0}(k)B_{0}(k)}{(1+(p-k)^{2})^{1/2}}\right\}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n\|\left(1-\Delta_{1}\right)^{1/2}q_{1}\phi^{(n)}\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq C\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}(1-\Delta)^{1/2}\chi\|,

where the supremum over
p\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
is finite by the same argument
as in (
6.6
), and where we further used

(6.79)

\displaystyle\|\left(1-\Delta_{1}\right)^{1/2}q_{1}\chi^{(n)}(t)\|\leq C\|u_{t}\|_{H^{1}}\|\left(1-\Delta_{1}\right)^{1/2}\chi^{(n)}(t)\|.

Adding up the relevant terms, we arrive at the desired estimate

(6.80)

\big|\big\langle\chi,H_{1}(t)\phi\big\rangle\big|\\
\leq C\|\left(\mathcal{N}+\mathbb{T}+1\right)^{1/2}\chi\|\Big(\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)\phi\|+\tfrac{1}{N}\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)\mathcal{N}_{b}^{1/2}\phi\|\Big).

The bound for
H_{2}(t)
is straightforward,

(6.81)

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\chi,H_{2}(t)\phi\big\rangle\big|\leq C\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\chi\|\,\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\phi\|,

where we used that
\|f_{u_{t}}+g_{u_{t},\alpha_{t}}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C
by Lemma

6.7
.

With the definition of the kernel of
H_{3}(t)
in (
6.67
), we
write

(6.82)

\displaystyle\big\langle\chi,H_{3}(t)\chi\big\rangle

\displaystyle\ =\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{2n}{\sqrt{N}}\int dk\,kB_{0}(k)\cdot 2\mathrm{Re}\big\langle q_{1}\chi^{(n)},e^{-ikx_{1}}(-i\nabla_{1}+F_{\alpha_{t}}(x_{1}))q_{1}\,a_{k}^{*}\chi^{(n)}\big\rangle

with
\chi^{(n)}\in\mathcal{F}^{(n)}\otimes\mathcal{F}
. With
\|F_{\alpha_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C
and
\|kB_{0}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}}\leq C
,
we obtain by Cauchy–Schwarz

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\chi,H_{3}(t)\chi\big\rangle\big|\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{4n}{\sqrt{N}}\|(-i\nabla_{1}+F_{\alpha_{t}}(x_{1}))q_{1}\chi^{(n)}\|\int dk\,|kB_{0}(k)|\,\|a_{k}\chi^{(n)}\|

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:nabla:q:Psi}}}{{\leq}}C\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{4n}{\sqrt{N}}\|(1-\Delta_{1})^{1/2}\chi^{(n)}\|\|\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})^{1/2}\chi^{(n)}\|

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:bound:dG:sqrt:omega}}}}{{\leq}}\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}(1-\Delta)^{1/2}\chi\|\,\|\mathcal{N}_{b}^{1/2}\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})^{1/2}\chi\|

(6.83)

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:bound:dG:sqrt:omega}}}{{\leq}}\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}(1-\Delta)^{1/2}\chi\|\,\|d\Gamma_{a}(\omega)^{1/2}\chi\|^{1/2}\,\|\mathcal{N}_{a}^{1/2}\mathcal{N}_{b}\chi\|^{1/2},

which implies the desired bound.

To bound
H_{4}(t)
, we apply Lemma
6.1
(for instance for the
term involving
b_{x}^{*}a_{k}^{*}a_{-l}
we choose
m_{b}=0,n_{b}=m_{a}=n_{a}=1
,

s=t=1/4
and
r_{a}=r_{b}=0
) and use from Lemma
6.7
that
\|N_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/4}\to L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}}+\|N_{t}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{1/4}^{\otimes 2}\to\mathbb{C}}\leq 2\|N_{t}\|_{L^{2}\to\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}^{\otimes 2}}\leq 2\|N_{t}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/8}^{\otimes 2}}\leq C
. This implies

(6.84)

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\chi,H_{4}(t)\chi\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big[\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)^{1/2}\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})^{1/2}\chi\|\|\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})^{1/2}\chi\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\|(1+\mathcal{N}_{b})^{\tfrac{1}{2}}(1+\mathcal{N}_{a})^{\tfrac{1}{2}}\chi\|\|(1+\mathcal{N}_{a})^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})\chi\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}_{a}+1)^{1/2}\chi\|\|(\mathcal{N}_{a}+1)^{-1/2}\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})\chi\|\Big].

The desired bound now follows easily from (
6.3a
).
For
H_{5}(t)
, we use that
\|Q_{t}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{1/4}\to L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}}+\|Q_{t}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{1/4}^{\otimes 2}\to L^{2}}\leq C\|Q_{t}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{1/8}^{\otimes 2}\to L^{2}}\leq C
by Lemma
6.7
, and Lemma
6.1
imply

(6.85)

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\chi,H_{5}(t)\chi\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{N}\Big[\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)^{1/2}\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})^{1/2}\chi\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)(\mathcal{N}_{a}+1)^{1/2}\chi\|\|(\mathcal{N}_{a}+1)^{-1/2}\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})\chi\|\Big].

The desired bound now follows from Lemma
6.2
.

Proof of (
6.70b
)
. The commutation
relations imply, e.g.,
[\mathcal{N},a_{k}]=-a_{k}
, so the non-zero terms in the
commutator
[\mathcal{N},H_{u,\alpha}^{\mathrm{D}}(t)]
have the same kernels, up to
signs, as those in
H_{u,\alpha}^{\mathrm{D}}(t)-\mathbb{T}
. They can thus be
estimated as in the proof of (
6.70a
), and we omit the
details.

Proof of (
6.70c
)
. This inequality is
obtained following similar steps as in the proof of
(
6.70a
) with some obvious modifications, like the use
of the bounds for the time-derivatives in Lemma
6.7
and
the use of
\|\dot{q}_{u_{t}}\|_{H^{1}}\leq C\sqrt{\rho(t)}
,
cf. Lemma.
6.6
.
∎

The next lemma shows that the fluctuation generator can be approximated by
the Bogoliubov Hamiltonian
\mathbb{H}^{\rm D}_{u,\alpha}(t)
for large
N
,
when tested on suitable states.

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
(u,\alpha)\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{5/2}
with

\|u\|_{L^{2}}=1
and
(u_{t},\alpha_{t})=\mathfrak{s}^{\rm D}[t](u,\alpha)

denote the solution to (
3.3
). There exists a constant

C>0
such that

(6.86)

\big|\big\langle\chi,\left(H^{\rm D}_{u,\alpha}(t)-\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha}^{\rm D}(t)\right)\phi\big\rangle\big|\\
\leq C\rho(t)N^{-1/2}\ln N\|\left(\mathcal{N}+\mathbb{T}+1\right)^{1/2}\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}^{3}+\mathbb{T}+1\right)^{1/2}\phi\|

for all
\chi\in\mathcal{F}^{\leq N}\otimes\mathcal{F}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}
, where
\rho(t)=\|u_{t}\|^{2}_{H^{3}}(1+\|\alpha_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{3/2}})^{2}
.

Proof.

Recalling the definitions of the fluctuation generator
(
6.68
) and the Bogoliubov Hamiltonian
(
6.49
), we write

\displaystyle\big\langle\chi,(H^{\rm D}_{u,\alpha}(t)-\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha}^{\rm D}(t))\phi\big\rangle

(6.87a)

\displaystyle=\big\langle\chi,\mathbb{K}^{(1)}_{u_{t}}\left(\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}\big]_{+}-1\right)\phi\big\rangle

(6.87b)

\displaystyle\ +\big\langle\chi,\left(\mathbb{K}^{(2)}_{u_{t}}\big(N^{-1}\sqrt{[(N-\mathcal{N}_{b})(N-\mathcal{N}_{b}-1)]_{+}}-1\big)+\text{h.c.}\right)\phi\big\rangle

(6.87c)

\displaystyle\ +\big\langle\chi,\Big(\int dxdk\,\big(q_{u_{t}}L_{\alpha_{t}}(k)u_{t}\big)(x)a^{*}_{k}b_{x}^{*}\Big(\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}\big]_{+}^{\frac{1}{2}}-1\Big)+\text{h.c.}\Big)\phi\big\rangle

(6.87d)

\displaystyle\ +\big\langle\chi,\Big(\int dxdk\,\big(q_{u_{t}}L_{\alpha_{t}}(k)^{*}u_{t}\big)(x)a_{k}b_{x}^{*}\Big(\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}\big]_{+}^{\frac{1}{2}}-1\Big)+\text{h.c.}\Big)\phi\big\rangle

(6.87e)

\displaystyle\ +\big\langle\chi,\int dkdl\,M_{u_{t}}(k,l)\mathcal{A}_{kl}\Big(\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}\big]_{+}-1\Big)\phi\big\rangle

(6.87f)

\displaystyle\ +\big\langle\chi,\left(H_{1}(t)+H_{2}(t)+H_{3}(t)+H_{4}(t)+H_{5}(t)\right)\phi\big\rangle

When bounding these terms, we have to take care to put any powers of the
number operator exceeding one-half to the right, i.e., on
\phi
. At the
same time, the power of
\mathcal{N}+\mathbb{T}
acting on
\phi
cannot exceed
one-half, either. For the terms in (
6.87a
)–(
6.87e
) the estimates are rather straightforward and given at the
end of the proof. The most difficult estimate is that for the term coming
from
H_{3}(t)
, which is also responsible for the presence of the factor

\ln N
in the statement.

Term
H_{3}(t)
. Recall the expression for the kernel
J_{u,\alpha}=2(q_{u}kB_{(\cdot)}(k)\cdot(-i\nabla+F_{\alpha})q_{u})
, which multiplies
b_{x}^{*}b_{y}a_{k}^{*}
in
H_{3}(t)
. This term is problematic, for when the gradient acts on
\phi
we cannot put further powers of
\mathcal{N}
on
\phi
while keeping control by
\|\left(\mathcal{N}^{3}+\mathbb{T}\right)^{1/2}\phi\|
.
We deal with this problem by using the identity

(6.88)

\displaystyle k\cdot B_{x_{1}}(k)\left(-i\nabla_{1}+F_{\alpha_{t}}(x_{1})\right)a^{*}_{k}

\displaystyle=\left(-i\nabla_{1}+F_{\alpha_{t}}(x_{1})\right)\cdot kB_{x_{1}}(k)+a^{*}_{k}k^{2}B_{x_{1}}(k)

and splitting the momentum integration into
|k|\leq\Lambda
and

|k|>\Lambda
. Together with the adjoint expression, which is less of a
problem, this gives

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\big\langle\chi,H_{3}(t)\phi\big\rangle

(6.89a)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n\big\langle\left(-i\nabla_{1}+F_{\alpha_{t}}(x_{1})\right)q_{1}\chi^{(n)},\int dk\,k\overline{B_{x_{1}}(k)}a_{k}q_{1}\phi^{(n)}\big\rangle

(6.89b)

\displaystyle\ +\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n\big\langle\chi^{(n)},q_{1}\int\limits_{\left|k\right|\geq\Lambda}dk\,kB_{x_{1}}(k)a_{k}^{*}\left(-i\nabla_{1}+F_{\alpha_{t}}(x_{1})\right)q_{1}\phi^{(n)}\big\rangle

(6.89c)

\displaystyle\ +\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n\big\langle\left(-i\nabla_{1}+F_{\alpha_{t}}(x_{1})\right)q_{1}\chi^{(n)},\int\limits_{\left|k\right|\leq\Lambda}dk\,kB_{x_{1}}(k)a_{k}^{*}q_{1}\phi^{(n)}\big\rangle

(6.89d)

\displaystyle\ +\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n\big\langle\int_{\left|k\right|\leq\Lambda}dk\,k^{2}\overline{B_{x_{1}}(k)}a_{k}q_{1}\chi^{(n)},q_{1}\phi^{(n)}\big\rangle.

In the first line, the gradient acts on
\chi
, so we can simply bound it as
in (
6.83
),

(6.90)

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:Norm approximation Gross transformed dynamics 2f1}|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-1/2}\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}(1-\Delta)^{1/2}\chi\|\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega)^{1/2}\phi\|^{1/2}\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}\phi\|^{1/2}.

In the second line, we can use that
\chi^{(n)}=0
for
n>N
to remove a
factor of
(n/N)^{1/2}
, since the lower cutoff
\Lambda
will give us a
small pre-factor. With the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Lemma

6.1
this gives

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:Norm approximation Gross transformed dynamics 2f2}|\leq\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n^{1/2}\Big|\big\langle\chi^{(n)},q_{1}\int_{\left|k\right|\geq\Lambda}dk\,kB_{x_{1}}(k)a_{k}^{*}\left(-i\nabla_{1}+F_{\alpha_{t}}(x_{1})\right)q_{1}\phi^{(n)}\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\ \leq C\Big(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Big\|\int_{\left|k\right|\geq\Lambda}dk\,k\overline{B_{x_{1}}(k)}a_{k}q_{1}\chi^{(n)}\Big\|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n\|\left(1-\Delta_{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{(n)}\|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\ \leq C\|\mathds{1}_{\left|\cdot\right|\geq\Lambda}\omega^{-1/2}kB_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega)^{1/2}\chi\|\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}(1-\Delta)^{1/2}\phi\|

(6.91)

\displaystyle\ \leq C\Lambda^{-1/2}\|\mathbb{T}^{1/2}\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}+\mathbb{T}\right)^{1/2}\phi\|,

where we used
\|\mathds{1}_{\left|\cdot\right|\geq\Lambda}\omega^{-1/2}kB_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\sqrt{4\pi/\Lambda}
in the last
step. Lemma
6.1
together with
\|\mathds{1}_{\left|\cdot\right|\leq\Lambda}kB_{0}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{4\pi\ln\Lambda}
yields for the third
line

(6.92)

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:Norm approximation Gross transformed dynamics 2f3}|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-1/2}CN^{-1/2}\sqrt{\ln\Lambda}\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{b}(1-\Delta)^{1/2}\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)\phi\|.

It remains to bound the last line, (
6.89d
). Here, we will need to use the regularity of

\chi
in
x
to improve the integrability of
k^{2}B_{x}(k)
. Using the
identity (
6.76
) to this end, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\int\limits_{\left|k\right|\leq\Lambda}dk\,k^{2}\overline{B_{x_{1}}(k)}a_{k}\chi\Big\|^{2}=\hskip-1.0pt\int\limits_{\left|k\right|\leq\Lambda}\hskip-1.0ptdk\hskip-1.0pt\int\limits_{\left|l\right|\leq\Lambda}\hskip-1.0ptdl\,k^{2}B_{0}(k)l^{2}B_{0}(l)\big\langle e^{ikx_{1}}a_{k}\chi,e^{ilx_{1}}a_{l}\chi\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{\left|k\right|\leq\Lambda}dk\int\limits_{\left|l\right|\leq\Lambda}dl\,\begin{aligned} k^{2}B_{0}(k)l^{2}B_{0}(l)\Big\langle&((-i\nabla_{1}-l)^{2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{ikx_{1}}(1-\Delta_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}a_{k}\chi,\\
&((-i\nabla_{1}-k)^{2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{ilx_{1}}(1-\Delta_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}a_{l}\chi\Big\rangle\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\ \leq\int_{\left|k\right|\leq\Lambda}dk\,\int_{\left|l\right|\leq\Lambda}dl\,k^{4}B_{0}(k)^{2}\|((-i\nabla_{1}-k)^{2}+1)^{-1/2}e^{ilx_{1}}(1-\Delta_{1})^{1/2}a_{l}\chi\|^{2}

(6.93)

\displaystyle\ \leq\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left\{\int_{\left|k\right|\leq\Lambda}dk\,\frac{k^{4}B_{0}(k)^{2}}{1+(p-k)^{2}}\right\}\|\mathcal{N}_{a}^{1/2}\left(1-\Delta_{1}\right)^{1/2}\chi\|^{2}.

By symmetric rearrangement (similarly as in
(
6.6
)) the supremum over
p\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
is
bounded by a constant times
\ln\Lambda
. With this inequality, we can
estimate the remaining term by

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:Norm approximation Gross transformed dynamics 2f4}|=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n\Big|\big\langle\int\limits_{\left|k\right|\leq\Lambda}dk\,k^{2}\overline{B_{x_{1}}(k)}a_{k}\mathcal{N}_{a}^{-\frac{1}{2}}q_{1}\chi^{(n)},q_{1}\left(\mathcal{N}_{a}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{(n)}\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\frac{\sqrt{\ln\Lambda}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n\|\left(1-\Delta_{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}q_{1}\chi^{(n)}\|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n\|\left(\mathcal{N}_{a}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{(n)}\|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(6.94)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\frac{\sqrt{\ln\Lambda}}{\sqrt{N}}\|\left(\mathcal{N}+\mathbb{T}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)\phi\|.

If we choose the cutoff parameter
\Lambda=N
we thus arrive at

(6.95)

\big|\big\langle\chi,H_{3}(t)\phi\big\rangle\big|\\
\leq CN^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\left(\mathcal{N}+\mathbb{T}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi\|\big(\|\left(\mathcal{N}^{3}+\mathbb{T}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi\|+\sqrt{\ln N}\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)\phi\|\big).

The terms
H_{j}(t)
for
j\neq 3
are somewhat easier to treat, since if there
are any gradients (as in
H_{2}(t)
, via
f_{u}
) they act on
u_{t}
and not

\chi,\phi
. The powers of
\omega
needed to render the kernels integrable
are strictly less than one, so the possibility of distributing factors of

\mathcal{N}
given by Lemma
6.1
is sufficient to treat theses terms,
as we now show.

Term

H_{1}(t)+H_{2}(t)
. For this contribution we can use the
already established bounds from the proof of Lemma

6.11
, that is, (
6.80
) and
(
6.81
), respectively.

Term
H_{4}(t)
. We use
\|N_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/8}\rightarrow L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/8}}+\|N_{t}\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1/8}^{\otimes 2}\rightarrow L^{2}}\leq\|N_{t}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/8}^{\otimes 2}}\leq C
(see Lemma

6.7
) and Lemma
6.1
to get

(6.96)

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\chi,H_{4}(t)\phi\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\begin{aligned} \Big[&\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi\|\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega^{\frac{1}{4}})\phi\|\\
&+\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)\phi\|\\
&+\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi\|\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi\|\Big].\end{aligned}

By means of (
6.3b
) we then obtain

(6.97)

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\chi,H_{4}(t)\phi\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-1/2}\|\left(\mathcal{N}+\mathbb{T}+1\right)^{1/2}\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}^{3}+\mathbb{T}+1\right)^{1/2}\phi\|.

Term
H_{5}(t)
. Recalling that
|k|B_{0}(k)\in\mathfrak{h}_{-s}
for
s>0
, Lemma
6.1
gives

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\chi,H_{5}(t)\phi\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-1}\Big[\|\mathcal{N}_{b}^{1/2}\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})^{1/2}\chi\|\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})^{1/2}\mathcal{N}_{b}^{1/2}\phi\|

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\|\mathcal{N}_{b}^{1/2}\left(\mathcal{N}_{a}+1\right)^{-1/2}\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)\phi\|

(6.98)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\|\mathcal{N}_{b}\chi\|\|\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega^{1/4})\phi\|\Big].

Since
\chi=\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{N}_{b}\leq N}\chi
,
Equation (
6.3b
) leads to

(6.99)

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\chi,H_{5}(t)\phi\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-1/2}\|\left(\mathcal{N}+\mathbb{T}+1\right)^{1/2}\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}^{3}+\mathbb{T}+1\right)^{1/2}\phi\|.

We conclude by estimating the terms from (
6.87a
)–(
6.87e
) by using that

(6.100a)

\displaystyle\pm\big(\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}\big]_{+}-1\big)

\displaystyle\leq N^{-1}\mathcal{N}_{b},

(6.100b)

\displaystyle\pm\big(N^{-1}\sqrt{[(N-\mathcal{N}_{b})(N-\mathcal{N}_{b}-1)]_{+}}-1\big)

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-1}\mathcal{N}_{b}.

Terms (
6.87a
) and (
6.87b
)
. Using (
6.72
), (
6.73
) and
\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{N}_{b}\leq N}\chi=\chi
,
we can estimate the first two lines by

(6.101)

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:Norm approximation Gross transformed dynamics 2b}|+|\eqref{eq:Norm approximation Gross transformed dynamics 2bb}|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-1/2}\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)^{1/2}\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)\phi\|.

Term (
6.87c
)
. Using Lemmas
6.7
and
6.1

we arrive at

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:Norm approximation Gross transformed dynamics 2c}|=2\Big|\mathrm{Re}\big\langle\chi,\int dxdk\,\ell^{(1)}(x,k)a^{*}_{k}b_{x}^{*}\Big(\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}\big]_{+}^{1/2}-1\Big)\phi\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:expansion of the number operators}}}}{{\leq}}\|\ell^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/2}}\|\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega)^{1/2}\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)^{1/2}([1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}]_{+}^{1/2}-1)\phi\|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\|\ell^{(1)}\|_{L^{2}\otimes\mathfrak{h}_{-1/4}}\|([1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}]_{+}^{1/2}-1)\chi\|\|\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})^{1/2}\mathcal{N}_{b}^{1/2}\phi\|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:expansion of the number operators}}}{{\leq}}CN^{-1}\|\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega)^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\phi\|+CN^{-1}\|\mathcal{N}_{b}\chi\|\|\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\sqrt{\omega})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}_{b}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi\|

(6.102)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:normbound:dGamma(omega^s/2)}}}{{\leq}}CN^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\|\text{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega)^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi\|+\|N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}_{b}\chi\|)(\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)^{3/2}\phi\|+\|\mathbb{T}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi\|).

This implies the claimed bound since
\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{N}_{b}\leq N}\chi=\chi
.

Term (
6.87d
)
. This term is treated in close analogy to the previous one,
leading to

(6.103)

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:Norm approximation Gross transformed dynamics 2d}|

\displaystyle\leq N^{-1/2}\|\left(\mathcal{N}+\mathbb{T}+1\right)^{1/2}\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}^{3}+\mathbb{T}+1\right)^{1/2}\phi\|.

Term (
6.87e
)
.
By means of Lemmas
6.7
and
6.1
, and
(
6.100a
) we get

(6.104)

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:Norm approximation Gross transformed dynamics 2e}|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-1}\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)^{1/2}\chi\|\|\left(\mathcal{N}+1\right)^{3/2}\phi\|.

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

6.5.
Estimates for the dressing
transformation

In this section, we will derive estimates for the fluctuation generator
associated with the dressing transformation
D_{u,\alpha}
defined in
(
3.3
), and its quadratic approximation

\mathbb{D}^{\Lambda}_{u,\alpha}
defined in (
4.10
).
We also give the proof of Lemma
3.6
.

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
\mathbb{D}^{\Lambda}_{u,\alpha}(\theta)
and
D_{u,\alpha}(\theta)
be defined by
(
4.10
) and (
3.3
).
There exists a constant
C>0
, such that for all
(u,\alpha)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{0}
with
\|u\|_{L^{2}}=1
,
|\theta|\leq 1
and
\Lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{\infty\}

\displaystyle\pm\mathbb{D}^{\Lambda}_{u,\alpha}(\theta)

\displaystyle\leq C(\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{0}})(\mathcal{N}+1)

\displaystyle\pm i[\mathcal{N},\mathbb{D}_{u,\alpha}^{\Lambda}(\theta)]

\displaystyle\leq C(\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{0}})(\mathcal{N}+1)

\displaystyle\pm\tfrac{d}{d\theta}\mathbb{D}_{u,\alpha}^{\Lambda}(\theta)

\displaystyle\leq C(\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{3/2}+\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{0}})(\mathcal{N}+1)

\displaystyle\pm i[\mathcal{N},D_{u,\alpha}(\theta)]

\displaystyle\leq C(\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{0}})(\mathcal{N}+1)(1+(\tfrac{1}{N}\mathcal{N}_{b})^{1/2})

in the sense of quadratic forms on
\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}
and

\displaystyle\big|\langle\phi,(D_{u,\alpha}(\theta)-\mathbb{D}_{u,\alpha}^{\infty}(\theta))\chi\rangle\big|\leq C\|\phi\|\,\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}\chi\|N^{-1/2}

for all
\phi,\chi\in\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathbb{D}_{u,\alpha}^{\Lambda}(\theta)
and

D_{u,\alpha}(\theta)
are defined w.r.t. the mean-field flow

(u^{\theta},\alpha^{\theta})=\mathfrak{D}[\theta](u,\alpha)
and that
|u^{\theta}|=|u|
by (
3.20
). One readily
shows that
\|\tau_{u,\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 3\|B_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\alpha\|_{L^{2}}
, and
\|\kappa^{\Lambda}_{u^{\theta}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\leq\|B_{0}\|_{L^{2}}
. By means of (
3.20
) and
(
6.13
) one further obtains

(6.105)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}\kappa^{\Lambda}_{u^{\theta}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}=\|\tau_{u,\alpha}\kappa_{u^{\theta}}^{\Lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\leq 3\|B_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|\alpha\|_{L^{2}}.

Using this, the estimates involving
\mathbb{D}^{\Lambda}_{u,\alpha}(\theta)

follow standard bounds for creation and annihilation operators that are
special cases of Lemma
6.1
.

Since
0\leq[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}]_{+}\leq 1
the commutator of

\mathcal{N}
with the first two terms in
(
3.3
) can be bounded as before. Now let
us denote the last term in (
3.3
), which is
cubic in the creation/annihilation operators, by

D_{u,\alpha}^{(3)}(\theta)
. Using the canonical commutation relations and
again standard estimates for creation and annihilation operators, one
obtains

(6.106)

\displaystyle\pm i[\mathcal{N},D_{u,\alpha}^{(3)}(\theta)]

\displaystyle\leq 4\|B_{0}\|_{L^{2}}N^{-1/2}\mathcal{N}_{b}\,\mathcal{N}_{a}^{1/2}.

This proves the bound on the commutator
[\mathcal{N},D_{u,\alpha}^{(3)}]
.

To show the last inequality, write

(6.107)

\displaystyle D_{u,\alpha}(\theta)-\mathbb{D}^{\infty}_{u,\alpha}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\int dxdk\Big(\kappa^{\infty}_{u^{\theta}}(k,x)a_{k}^{*}-\kappa^{\infty}_{u^{\theta}}(-k,x)a_{k}\Big)b_{x}^{*}\big(\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N}]_{+}^{1/2}-1\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\int dxdk\Big(\overline{\kappa^{\infty}_{u^{\theta}}(k,x)}a_{k}-\overline{\kappa^{\infty}_{u^{\theta}}(-k,x)}a_{k}^{*}\Big)b_{x}\big(\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}-1}{N}]_{+}^{1/2}-1\big)+D_{u,\alpha}^{(3)}(\theta).

It is straightforward to show that

(6.108)

|\langle\phi,D_{u,\alpha}^{(3)}(\theta)\chi\rangle|\leq 4\|B_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\phi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{b}\,\mathcal{N}_{a}^{1/2}\chi\|N^{-1/2}.

For the first two terms, the bounds
\|\kappa^{\infty}_{u^{\theta}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})}\leq\|B_{0}\|_{{L^{2}}}
and
([1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}-j}{N}]_{+}^{1/2}-1)^{2}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)
for
j\in\{0,1\}
imply that

(6.109)

\Big|\langle\phi,\int dxdk\,\Big(\kappa^{\infty}_{u^{\theta}}(k,x)a_{k}^{*}-\kappa^{\infty}_{u^{\theta}}(-k,x)a_{k}\Big)b_{x}^{\bullet}\big(\big[1-\tfrac{\mathcal{N}_{b}-j}{N}]_{+}^{1/2}-1\big)\chi\rangle\Big|\\
\leq C\|\phi\|\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+1)^{1/2}\mathcal{N}_{b}^{1/2}\mathcal{N}_{a}^{1/2}\chi\|N^{-1/2}

where
\bullet\in\{\varnothing,*\}
. This completes the proof of the
lemma.
∎

We now turn to the proof of Lemma
3.6
, which
relates the energy of excitations in
W^{\mathrm{D}}\Psi_{N}
to the difference
of the energy of
\Psi
to its mean-field energy. As explained below the
statement of the lemma, it is not possible to apply the strategy of the proof
of Theorem
3.2

because
\mathbb{T}
is not dominated by the generator
D_{u,\alpha}(\theta)

in (
3.3
). Instead, our proof relies on
comparing
X_{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}\mathbb{T}X_{\mathfrak{D}}
directly with the
difference between the many-body energy per particle and the dressed
mean-field energy
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, evaluated at
\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha)
. Energy estimates of this kind were previously used in a
different context in
[
56
]
.

Proof of Lemma
3.6
.

We recall Lemma
3.1
and the fact that

\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{1}\circ\mathfrak{D}
as shown by Equation
(
5.5
) for
\mathcal{E}_{0}=\mathcal{E}
. With this at hand, we write the difference between the many-body energy
per particle and the mean-field energy as

(6.110)

\displaystyle N^{-1}\big\langle\Psi_{N},H_{N}\Psi_{N}\big\rangle-\mathcal{E}(u,\alpha)=N^{-1}\big\langle W^{\rm D}\Psi_{N},H_{N}^{\rm D}W^{\rm D}\Psi_{N}\big\rangle-\mathcal{E}_{1}\circ\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha).

Moreover, for
\zeta={X}_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha)}W^{\rm D}\Psi
, we can use (
2.15b
) to write the relevant
\gamma

functional as

(6.111)

\displaystyle\gamma\big[W^{\rm D}\Psi_{N},\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha)\big]=N^{-1}\big\langle\zeta,\mathbb{T}\zeta\big\rangle.

To relate the expressions on the right-hand side of (
6.110
) and (
6.111
), we
make use of the excitation map
X_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha)}
. To do so, we
rewrite
H_{N}^{\rm D}
in terms of the fluctuation generator
H_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha)}^{\rm D}(0)
from (
6.68
). This will
allow us to employ previously established estimates.

To ease up the notation, we set from now on

(u^{\mathrm{D}},\alpha^{\mathrm{D}})=\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha)
and the
shorthand
q=q_{u^{\mathrm{D}}}
,
h=h_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha)}
,

f=f_{u^{\mathrm{D}}}
,
g=g_{u^{\mathrm{D}},\alpha^{\mathrm{D}}}
(see
(
3.4a
)–(
3.4f
) for the definitions
of these objects). We can employ the results from Appendix

C
to obtain

\displaystyle X_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha)}H_{N}^{\rm D}X_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha)}^{*}

\displaystyle=H^{\rm D}_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha)}(0)+\big\langle u^{\mathrm{D}},hu^{\mathrm{D}}\big\rangle\big(N-\mathcal{N}_{b}\big)+N\big\langle\alpha^{\mathrm{D}},\omega\alpha^{\mathrm{D}}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{N}\Phi\big(\omega\alpha^{\mathrm{D}}+f+g\big)-b^{*}(u^{\mathrm{D}})b(qhu^{\mathrm{D}})

(6.112)

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{N-\mathcal{N}_{b}}b(qhu^{\mathrm{D}})+b^{*}(qhu^{\mathrm{D}})\sqrt{N-\mathcal{N}_{b}}.

From the first inequality of Lemma
6.11
, the
fact that
\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{N}_{b}\leq N}\zeta=\zeta
, and (
6.100a
), we get

(6.113)

\displaystyle\langle\zeta,\mathbb{T}\zeta\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2\langle\zeta,H_{\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha)}^{\rm D}(0)\zeta\rangle+C\langle\zeta,\mathcal{N}\zeta\rangle.

With the formula (
5.3
) for the dressed mean-field
energy
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, we arrive at

(6.114)

\displaystyle N^{-1}\big\langle\zeta,\mathbb{T}\zeta\big\rangle-\left|N^{-1}\big\langle\Psi_{N},H_{N}\Psi_{N}\big\rangle-\mathcal{E}(u,\alpha)\right|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-1}\langle\zeta,\mathcal{N}\zeta\rangle+\Big|-N^{-1}\big\langle u^{\mathrm{D}},hu^{\mathrm{D}}\big\rangle\big\langle\zeta,\mathcal{N}_{b}\zeta\big\rangle-N^{-1}\big\langle\zeta,b^{*}(u^{\mathrm{D}})b(qhu^{\mathrm{D}})\zeta\big\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad+N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big\langle\zeta,\Phi\big(\omega\alpha^{\mathrm{D}}+f+g\big)\zeta\big\rangle+2N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{Re}\big\langle\zeta,\sqrt{1-\mathcal{N}_{b}/N}b(qhu^{\mathrm{D}})\zeta\big\rangle\Big|.

We bound the terms on the right hand side of in (
6.114
) by (using that
\|\zeta\|=\|\Psi_{N}\|=1
)

(6.115)

|\eqref{eq:energy difference rest}|\leq C\big\langle\zeta,\mathcal{N}\zeta\big\rangle N^{-1}\big(1+\|hu^{\mathrm{D}}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\\
+CN^{-1/2}\big(\|\omega\alpha^{\mathrm{D}}+f+g\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{a}^{1/2}\zeta\|+C\|\mathcal{N}_{b}^{1/2}\zeta\|\big).

By Lemmas
6.4
and
6.5
, the norms
of
f
,
g
,
hu^{\mathrm{D}}
are bounded in terms of the

H^{2}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{1/2}
-norm of
(u^{\mathrm{D}},\alpha^{\mathrm{D}})
,
which by Lemma
3.3
is controlled by the norm
of
(u,\alpha)\in H^{2}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{3/2}
. Thus there exists a
constant
C
, depending on this norm, so that

(6.116)

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:energy difference rest}|\leq C\big(N^{-1}\big\langle\zeta,\mathcal{N}\zeta\big\rangle+(N^{-1}\big\langle\zeta,\mathcal{N}\zeta\big\rangle)^{1/2}\big).

Moreover by (
2.15a
) and Lemma
3.5
for
\theta=1
, we have

(6.117)

\displaystyle N^{-1}\big\langle\zeta,\mathcal{N}\zeta\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\beta\big[W^{\rm D}\Psi_{N},\mathfrak{D}(u,\alpha)\big]\leq C\big(\beta\big[\Psi_{N},(u,\alpha)\big]+N^{-1}\big).

Combined with (
6.116
) this proves the statement of the
Lemma.
∎

Appendix A
Initial states

Proof of Proposition
1.2
.

Let
W^{\rm D}_{\geq K}=W(N^{-1/2}\sum_{j=1}^{N}B_{K,x_{j}})
and
W^{\rm D}_{\geq K,x_{j}}=W(N^{-1/2}B_{K,x_{j}})
. The first inequality of the
Proposition can be obtained similarly as
[
61
, Prop. II.2]
. More
explicitly, we use (
2.8
) and (
2.9
) to estimate

\displaystyle N^{-1}\|\mathcal{N}_{a}^{1/2}W^{*}(\sqrt{N}\alpha)\Psi_{N,K}\|

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\int d^{3}k\,\|a_{k}(W^{\rm D}_{\geq K})^{*}(u^{\otimes N}\otimes\Omega)\|^{2}

(A.1)

\displaystyle\leq\|B_{K,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq CK^{-2}.

By means of

(A.2)

\displaystyle W^{\rm D}_{\geq K}(q_{u})_{1}(W^{\rm D}_{\geq K})^{*}

\displaystyle=(q_{u})_{1}+|u\rangle\langle u|_{1}-W^{\rm D}_{\geq K,x_{1}}|u\rangle\langle u|_{1}(W^{\rm D}_{\geq K,x_{1}})^{*}

and

(A.3)

\displaystyle\big(1-(W^{\rm D}_{\geq K,x_{1}})\big)\big(1-(W^{\rm D}_{\geq K,x_{1}})^{*}\big)\leq N^{-1}\hat{\Phi}(iB_{K,x_{1}})^{2}

we get

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle\Psi_{N,K},(q_{u})_{1}\Psi_{N,K}\big\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\left(1-(W^{\rm D}_{\geq K,x_{1}})^{*}\right)u^{\otimes N}\otimes W(\sqrt{N}\alpha)\Omega\|

\displaystyle\leq 2N^{-1/2}\|B_{K,0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\big(\mathcal{N}_{a}+1\big)^{1/2}u^{\otimes N}\otimes W(\sqrt{N}\alpha)\Omega\|

(A.4)

\displaystyle\leq CK^{-1}\big(N^{-1/2}+\|\alpha\|_{L^{2}}\big),

and thus
\beta[\Psi_{N,K},u,\alpha]\leq CK^{-1}\left(1+\|\alpha\|_{L^{2}}\right)
.
Since
W^{\rm D}_{\geq K}=\prod_{j=1}^{N}W^{\rm D}_{\geq K,x_{j}}
, the
transformation relations of the dressing transformation from
[
46
, Section
II]
lead to

\displaystyle W^{\rm D}_{\geq K}H_{N}(W^{\rm D}_{\geq K})^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\Big[-\Delta_{j}+N^{-1/2}\hat{\Phi}(\mathds{1}(\left|\cdot\right|\leq K)G_{x_{j}})

\displaystyle\quad+N^{-1}\big(a(kB_{K,x_{j}})^{2}+\text{h.c.}+2a^{*}(kB_{K,x_{j}})a(kB_{K,x_{j}})\big)

\displaystyle\quad-2N^{-1/2}\big(i\nabla_{x_{j}}\cdot a(kB_{K,x_{j}})+a^{*}(kB_{K,x_{j}})\cdot i\nabla_{x_{j}}\big)\Big]

(A.5)

\displaystyle\quad+N^{-1}\sum_{i<j}V_{K}(x_{i}-x_{j})+\textnormal{d}\Gamma_{a}(\omega)+E_{K}

with

(A.6)

\displaystyle V_{K}(x_{i}-x_{j})

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{Re}\big\langle B_{K,x_{i}},\omega B_{K,x_{j}}\big\rangle-4\mathrm{Re}\big\langle G_{x_{i}},B_{K,x_{j}}\big\rangle

and

(A.7)

\displaystyle E_{K}=\int_{\left|k\right|\leq K}\frac{dk}{\omega(k)\left(k^{2}+\omega(k)\right)}.

The shifting property of the Weyl operator (
2.9
) then lets write the expectation value of the energy per
particle as

(A.8)

N^{-1}\big\langle\Psi_{N,K},H_{N}\Psi_{N,K}\big\rangle=N^{-1}E_{K}\mathcal{E}(u,\alpha)\\
+\Big\langle u,\big(2\mathrm{Re}\big\langle G_{(\cdot)},\alpha_{\geq K}\big\rangle+A_{\alpha_{\geq K},(\cdot)}+F_{\alpha_{\geq K}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}V_{K}*\left|u\right|^{2}\big)u\Big\rangle

with
\alpha_{\geq K}=\mathds{1}_{\left|\cdot\right|\geq K}\alpha
and
A_{\alpha}
,

F_{\alpha}
as defined in (
3.4b
),
(
3.4c
). Note that
\left|E_{K}\right|\leq C\left(1+\ln K\right)
. By means of
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left|\big\langle kB_{x},\alpha_{\geq K}\big\rangle\right|\leq CK^{-1}\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1}}
,

\|V_{K}\|\leq CK^{-3/2}
and
\|u\|_{L^{2}}=1
we get

(A.9)

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle u,A_{\alpha_{\geq K},(\cdot)}u\big\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq CK^{-1}\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1}}\|u\|_{H^{1}},\quad\|F_{\alpha_{\geq K}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq CK^{-1}\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1}}

and

(A.10)

\displaystyle\|V_{K}*\left|u\right|^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\|V_{K}\|_{L^{2}}\|\left|u\right|^{2}\|_{L^{2}}\leq CK^{-3/2}\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

Inequality (
6.9
) with
G_{(\cdot)}\mapsto\omega^{-1/2}G_{(\cdot)}
and
\alpha\mapsto\omega^{1/2}\alpha
leads to

(A.11)

\|\mathrm{Re}\big\langle G_{(\cdot)},\alpha_{\geq K}\big\rangle u\|_{L^{2}}\leq CK^{-1}\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1}}\|u\|_{H^{1}}.

In total, we obtain

(A.12)

\left|N^{-1}\big\langle\Psi_{N,K},H_{N}\Psi_{N,K}\big\rangle-\mathcal{E}(u,\alpha)\right|\\
\leq C\left(K^{-1}+N^{-1}(1+\ln K)\right)\left(\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\alpha\|_{\mathfrak{h}_{1}}^{2}\right).

∎

Appendix B
Bogoliubov transformations

For a linear map
T
on a complex Hilbert space, we denote by
\bar{T}f=\overline{T\bar{f}}
its complex conjugate.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\mathscr{H}
be a Hilbert space and
\mathbb{U}_{n}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
be a family of unitary Bogoliubov transformations on the Fock
space over
\mathscr{H}
, that is, there exist
\mathfrak{u}_{n}
linear,
bounded and
\mathfrak{v}_{n}
Hilbert Schmidt, so that

\mathbb{U}_{n}^{*}a^{*}(f)\mathbb{U}_{n}=a^{*}(\mathfrak{u}_{n}f)+a(\mathfrak{v}_{n}\bar{f}),\qquad\mathbb{U}_{n}^{*}a(f)\mathbb{U}_{n}=a(\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{n}f)+a^{*}(\overline{\mathfrak{v}_{n}f}).

Assume that

\mathbb{U}_{\infty}:=\slim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{U}_{n}

exists, and moreover there exists a self-adjoint
A
,
D(A)
with
A\geq 1

and
C>0
so that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
and
\Psi\in D(\textnormal{d}\Gamma(A)^{1/2})
it holds

\langle\mathbb{U}_{n}\Psi,\mathcal{N}\mathbb{U}_{n}\Psi\rangle\leq C\langle\Psi,(1+\textnormal{d}\Gamma(A))\Psi\rangle.

Then
\mathbb{U}_{\infty}
is a Bogoliubov transformation and the
corresponding maps
\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{v}
satisfy
\|\mathfrak{u}\|_{\mathscr{H}\to\mathscr{H}}\leq C+1
,
\|\mathfrak{v}\|_{\mathfrak{S}_{2}(\mathscr{H})}\leq C
.

Proof.

We start by showing that for
\Psi,\Phi\in D(\mathrm{d}\Gamma(A)^{1/2})
,

f\in\mathscr{H}
, and
\bullet\in\{\varnothing,*\}

(B.1)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\langle\Phi,\mathbb{U}_{n}^{*}a^{\bullet}(f)\mathbb{U}_{n}\Psi\rangle=\langle\Phi,\mathbb{U}_{\infty}^{*}a^{\bullet}(f)\mathbb{U}_{\infty}\Psi\rangle.

To see this, note that

(B.2)

\displaystyle\Big|\langle\Phi,\mathbb{U}_{n}^{*}a^{\bullet}(f)\mathbb{U}_{n}\Psi\rangle-\langle\Phi,\mathbb{U}_{\infty}^{*}a^{\bullet}(f)\mathbb{U}_{\infty}\Psi\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\begin{aligned} C\|f\|_{\mathscr{H}}\Big(&\|(\mathbb{U}_{n}-\mathbb{U}_{\infty})\Phi\|_{\mathcal{F}}\|(1+\mathrm{d}\Gamma(A))^{1/2}\Psi\|_{\mathcal{F}}\\
&+\|(\mathbb{U}_{n}-\mathbb{U}_{\infty})\Psi\|_{\mathcal{F}}\|(1+\mathrm{d}\Gamma(A))^{1/2}\Phi\|_{\mathcal{F}}\Big),\end{aligned}

which tends to zero since
\mathbb{U}_{n}
converges strongly to

\mathbb{U}_{\infty}
.

Now let
f\in D(A)
, so
a^{*}(f)\Omega\in D(\mathrm{d}\Gamma(A)^{1/2})
. Then we have, using that
\mathbb{U}_{n}
is a
Bogoliubov transformation for
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\langle a^{*}(f)\Omega,\mathbb{U}_{\infty}a^{*}(g)\mathbb{U}_{\infty}^{*}\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\langle a^{*}(f)\Omega,(a^{*}(\mathfrak{u}_{n}g)+a(\mathfrak{v}_{n}\bar{g}))\Omega\rangle

(B.3a)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\langle f,\mathfrak{u}_{n}g\rangle,

\displaystyle\langle a^{*}(f)\Omega,\mathbb{U}_{\infty}a(g)\mathbb{U}_{\infty}^{*}\Omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\langle a^{*}(f)\Omega,(a(\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{n}g)+a^{*}(\overline{\mathfrak{v}_{n}g}))\Omega\rangle

(B.3b)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\langle f,\overline{\mathfrak{v}_{n}g}\rangle.

Since moreover

(B.4)

|\langle a^{*}(f)\Omega,\mathbb{U}_{\infty}a^{\bullet}(g)\mathbb{U}_{\infty}^{*}\Omega\rangle|\leq\|f\|_{\mathscr{H}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{H}}\overbrace{\|(1+\mathcal{N}^{1/2})\mathbb{U}_{\infty}\Omega\|_{\mathcal{F}}}^{\leq C+1},

the operators
\mathfrak{u}_{n},\mathfrak{v}_{n}
converge weakly to operators

\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{v}
with norm less than
C+1
. Weak convergence
of
\mathfrak{u}_{n}
,
\mathfrak{v}_{n}
, implies that for
\Phi\in D(\mathcal{N}^{1/2})
,
\Psi\in\mathcal{F}
, and
f\in\mathscr{H}

(B.5)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\langle\Phi,a(\mathfrak{u}_{n}f)\Psi\rangle=\langle\Phi,a(\mathfrak{u}f)\Psi\rangle,\qquad\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\langle\Phi,a(\mathfrak{v}_{n}f)\Psi\rangle=\langle\Phi,a(\mathfrak{v}f)\Psi\rangle,

and thus for
\Phi,\Psi\in D(\mathcal{N}^{1/2})

(B.6)

\langle\Phi,\mathbb{U}_{\infty}^{*}a^{*}(f)\mathbb{U}_{\infty}\Psi\rangle=\langle a(\mathfrak{u}f)\Phi,\Psi\rangle+\langle\Phi,a(\mathfrak{v}\bar{f})\Psi\rangle=\langle\Phi,(a^{*}(\mathfrak{u}f)+a(\mathfrak{v}\bar{f}))\Psi\rangle,

and similarly for
a(f)
. Moreover, we have

(B.7)

\|\mathfrak{v}_{n}\|_{\mathfrak{S}_{2}}^{2}=\|\mathcal{N}^{1/2}\mathbb{U}_{n}\Omega\|^{2}\leq C,

so the sequence
\mathfrak{v}_{n}
is bounded in
\mathfrak{S}_{2}
, whence it
has a subsequence that converges weakly in
\mathfrak{S}_{2}
. Since
\langle f,\mathfrak{v}_{n}g\rangle=\mathrm{Tr}(|f\rangle\langle g|\mathfrak{v}_{n})
, the limit must be
\mathfrak{v}
, so
\mathfrak{v}\in\mathfrak{S}_{2}
with norm less than
C
. This proves the claim.
∎

Lemma B.2
.

The unitaries
\mathbb{W}^{\Lambda}_{u,\alpha}(\theta)
and
\mathbb{U}_{u,\alpha,1}^{\Lambda}(t)
defined in Propositions

4.10
and
5.2
are Bogoliubov
transformations under the hypothesis given there.

Proof.

In view of the strong convergence of
\mathbb{U}^{\Lambda}_{u,\alpha,1}(t)

to
\mathbb{U}^{\infty}_{u,\alpha,1}(t)
and the bound from
Proposition
5.2
(i), it is sufficient it prove the claim on

\mathbb{U}_{u,\alpha,1}
for
\Lambda<\infty
. For
\mathbb{W}^{\Lambda}_{u,\alpha}
such a distinction is not necessary. Note that the
terms in the generators
\mathbb{D}^{\infty}_{u,\alpha}
and

\mathbb{H}_{u,\alpha,1}^{\Lambda}
,
\Lambda<\infty
of these unitaries with
two creation operators (
b^{*}
and/or
a^{*}
) have coefficients that are
square integrable functions of their arguments (compare
Lemma
6.7
). Since this is the only relevant property, we
give an exemplary proof in the case of
\mathbb{W}_{u,\alpha}^{\infty}
.

As a first step, consider the (tentative) equations for the operators

\mathfrak{u}(t)
,
\mathfrak{v}(t)
associated with a Bogoliubov
transformation. These are usually expressed in terms of the matrix

(B.8)

\mathcal{V}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{u}&\mathfrak{v}\\
\overline{\mathfrak{v}}&\overline{\mathfrak{u}}\end{pmatrix}\,

where, in our case,
\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{v}:L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\oplus L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\oplus L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
are linear and bounded. For the
case of
\mathbb{W}_{u,\alpha}^{\infty}
, asking that

(B.9)

i\partial_{\theta}\big(c^{*}(\mathfrak{u}(\theta)(f\oplus g))+c(\mathfrak{v}(\theta)(\bar{f}\oplus\bar{g}))\big)=[\mathbb{D}_{u,\alpha}^{\infty}(\theta),c^{*}(\mathfrak{u}(\theta)(f\oplus g))+c(\mathfrak{v}(\theta)(\bar{f}\oplus\bar{g}))]

yields the equations for
\mathcal{V}

(B.10)

i\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{V}(\theta)=\mathcal{A}(\theta)\mathcal{V}(\theta),

with

(B.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}A_{\mathrm{d}}&-A_{\mathrm{o}}\\
\bar{A}_{\mathrm{o}}&-\bar{A}_{\mathrm{d}}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle A_{\mathrm{o}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\kappa_{u^{\theta}}^{\infty}(\cdot,x)\\
\kappa_{u^{\theta}}^{\infty}(k,\cdot)&0\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\quad A_{\mathrm{d}}=\begin{pmatrix}\tau_{u,\alpha}&-\kappa_{u^{\theta}}^{\infty}(-(\cdot),x)\\
-\overline{\kappa}_{u^{\theta}}^{\infty}(-k,\cdot)&0\end{pmatrix},

where
\kappa
acts as an integral operator by integrating in the variable
denoted by
(\cdot)
. These equations admit a unique solution

\mathcal{V}(\theta)
with
\mathcal{V}(0)=1
(
\mathcal{A}
is a bounded
perturbation of the diagonal terms, which are generators).

Since
\kappa_{u^{\theta}}^{\infty}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{6})
, the off-diagonal part

A_{\mathrm{o}}
is a Hilbert-Schmidt operator, and consequently the
off-diagonal part
\mathfrak{v}(\theta)
of
\mathcal{V}(\theta)
is also
Hilbert-Schmidt
[
12
, Lem.4.9]
. By Shale-Stinespring
criterion (see, e.g.,
[
12
, Lem. 4.2]
) there thus exists a
Bogoliubov transformation
\mathbb{V}(\theta)
associated to

\mathfrak{u}(\theta)
,
\mathfrak{v}(\theta)
,
i.e.
\mathcal{V}(\theta)
. This transformation is determined up to a

\theta
-dependent phase. We can fix this phase by asking that

\mathbb{V}(\theta)\Omega=\mathbb{W}_{0}^{\infty}(\theta)\Omega
, as we now
show.

The vacuum vector
\Omega
spans the one-dimensional space on which

c(f\oplus g)=b(f)+a(g)
vanish for all
f,g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Then

\mathbb{V}(\theta)\Omega
spans the joint kernel of
\mathbb{V}(\theta)c(f\oplus g)\mathbb{V}(\theta)^{*}
. Using the equation satisfied by

\mathcal{V}(\theta)^{-1}
, we find (since we do not know a priori that

\mathbb{V}
is differentiable,
i\partial_{\theta}\mathbb{V}(\theta)

denotes the weak derivative)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=i\partial_{\theta}\big(\mathbb{V}(\theta)c(f\oplus g)\mathbb{V}(\theta)^{*}\big)\mathbb{V}(\theta)\Omega

(B.12)

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbb{V}(\theta)c(f\oplus g)\mathbb{V}(\theta)^{*}\big)i\partial_{\theta}\mathbb{V}(\theta)\Omega+[\mathbb{V}(\theta)c(f\oplus g)\mathbb{V}(\theta)^{*},\mathbb{D}_{0}^{\infty}(\theta)]\mathbb{V}(\theta)\Omega.

It follows that

(B.13)

i\partial_{\theta}\mathbb{V}(\theta)\Omega-\mathbb{D}_{0}^{\infty}(\theta)\mathbb{V}(\theta)\Omega

is in the kernel of
\mathbb{V}(\theta)c(f\oplus g)\mathbb{V}(\theta)^{*}
,
and thus proportional to
\mathbb{V}(\theta)\Omega
. The constant of
proportionality must be real, since
\mathbb{V}(\theta)
is unitary, and
thus we can set it to zero by adjusting the phase, i.e., setting

\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\theta)=\mathbb{V}(\theta)e^{i\int_{0}^{\theta}\nu(s)ds}
, which is also a Bogoliubov transformation associated with

\mathcal{V}(\theta)
. By uniqueness of the solution proved in
Proposition
4.3
, we thus have

\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\theta)\Omega=\mathbb{W}^{\infty}_{0}(\theta)\Omega
.
Using the explicit action of
\mathbb{V}(\theta)
on the creation and
annihilation operators, one shows by induction that

\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\theta)\Psi=\mathbb{W}_{0}^{\infty}(\theta)
for any
state
\Psi
obtained by application of a finite number of creation and
annihilation operators (see the proof of
[
12
, Lem.4.8]
).
Since the span of such
\Psi
is dense, this proves equality and thus that
